31-25-00-710-803Clocks - System Test ATA 31 · Task 710-803

Task Description

2. Clocks - System Test

A. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

B. System Test

SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-100 (1) Set the battery switch, on the P5 overhead panel, to the ON position. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-018 (2) Open and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-020 (3) Make sure that the time shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-021 (4) Make sure that the UTC-TIME indication shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-023 (5) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-024 (6) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-025 (7) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-058 (8) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-026 (9) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. (a) Make sure that the date shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-027 (10) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. (a) Make sure that the time shows. 31-25-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH GPS CLOCKS WITH SET BUTTON (Continued) SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-028 (11) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY MO/YR. (a) Make sure that the date shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-029 (12) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-030 (13) Push the SET button to allow for the setting of the hours digits. (a) Push the + or - buttons to adjust the hours digits to 12:00. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-031 (14) Push the SET button to allow for the setting of the minutes digits. (a) Push the + or - buttons to adjust the minutes digits to 12:10. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-032 (15) Push the SET button to complete the setting of time. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-033 (16) Open this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-034 (17) Make sure that the displays are blank. (a) Wait at least 2 minutes before continue to the next step. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-035 (18) Close this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-036 (19) Make sure that the MAN-TIME has continued to increment the previous time set. NOTE: The 2 minutes interruption of the clock display does not affect the clock operation. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-037 (20) Open this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-038 (21) Make sure that the MAN TIME display continues to show time. 31-25-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH GPS CLOCKS WITH SET BUTTON (Continued) SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-039 (22) Close this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-040 (23) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-041 (24) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-042 (25) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-043 (26) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-044 (27) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. (a) Make sure that the MAN-TIME display continues to show the manually set time. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-045 (28) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-046 (29) Push the SET button to allow for the setting of the DAY digits. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-047 (30) Push the + or - buttons to adjust the DAY digits to the current day. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-048 (31) Push the SET button to allow for the setting of the MON digits. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-049 (32) Push the + or - buttons to adjust the MON digits to the current month. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-050 (33) Push the SET button to allow for the setting of the YR digits. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-051 (34) Push the + or - buttons to adjust the YR digits to the current year. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-052 (35) Push the SET button to complete the setting of the date. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-053 (36) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-054 (37) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-055 (38) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-056 (39) Make sure that the MAN-DAY MO/YR display continues to show manually set date. 31-25-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH GPS CLOCKS WITH SET BUTTON (Continued) SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-101 (40) Set the battery switch, on the P5 overhead panel, to the OFF position.

Task Description

3. Clocks - System Test

B. System Test

SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-102 (1) Set the battery switch, on the P5 overhead panel, to the ON position. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-060 (2) Open and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-062 (3) Make sure that the time shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-063 (4) Make sure that the UTC-TIME indication shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-064 (5) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-065 (6) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-066 (7) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-067 (8) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-068 (9) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. (a) Make sure that the date shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-069 (10) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. (a) Make sure that the time shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-070 (11) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY MO/YR. 31-25-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH GPS CLOCKS WITH SET CONTROL KNOB (Continued) (a) Make sure that the date shows. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-071 (12) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-072 (13) Push the SET control knob to allow for the setting of the hours digits. (a) Turn the knob clockwise or counterclockwise to adjust the hour digits to 12:00. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-073 (14) Push the SET control knob to allow for the setting of the minute digits. (a) Turn the knob clockwise or counterclockwise to adjust the minute digits to 12:10. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-074 (15) Push the SET control knob to complete the setting of time. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-075 (16) Open this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-076 (17) Make sure that the displays are blank. (a) Wait at least 2 minutes before continue to the next step. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-077 (18) Close this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-078 (19) Make sure that the MAN-TIME has continued to increment the previous time set. NOTE: The 2 minutes interruption of the clock display does not affect the clock operation. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-099 (20) Open this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-079 (21) Make sure that the MAN TIME display continues to show time. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-080 (22) Close this circuit breaker: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK 31-25-00 RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH GPS CLOCKS WITH SET CONTROL KNOB (Continued) SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-081 (23) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-082 (24) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-083 (25) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-084 (26) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-085 (27) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-TIME. (a) Make sure that the MAN-TIME display continues to show the manually set time. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-086 (28) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-087 (29) Push the SET control knob to allow for the setting of the DAY digits. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-088 (30) Turn the knob clockwise or counterclockwise to adjust the DAY digits to the current day. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-089 (31) Push the SET control knob for the setting of the MON digits. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-090 (32) Turn the knob clockwise or counterclockwise to adjust the MON digits to the current month. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-091 (33) Push the SET control knob to allow for the setting of the YR digits. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-092 (34) Turn the knob clockwise or counterclockwise to adjust the YR digits to the current year. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-093 (35) Push the SET control knob to complete the setting of the date. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-094 (36) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the MAN-DAY and MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-095 (37) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-TIME. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-096 (38) Push the TIME/DATE button to show the UTC-DAY MO/YR. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-097 (39) Make sure that the MAN-DAY MO/YR display continues to show manually set date. SUBTASK 31-25-00-860-103 (40) Set the battery switch, on the P5 overhead panel, to the OFF position.

31-25-11-000-801Clock Removal ATA 31 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Clock Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) This task has the steps to remove the clock.

B. References

Reference Title 20-40-12-000-802 ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Clock Removal

SUBTASK 31-25-11-860-001 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK SUBTASK 31-25-11-040-001 CAUTION DO NOT TOUCH THE CONNECTOR PINS, OR OTHER CONDUCTORS. IF YOU TOUCH THESE CONDUCTORS, ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE COMPONENTS. (2) Before the clock [1] is touched, do this task: ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802. SUBTASK 31-25-11-020-001 (3) Remove the clock [1]: (a) Loosen the screws [2] and screws [3] that hold the clock [1] to the mounting clamp. (b) Pull the clock [1] out of the instrument panel. (c) Disconnect the electrical connector. SUBTASK 31-25-11-860-002 (4) Put protective covers on the electrical connector.

31-25-11-400-801Clock Installation ATA 31 · Task 400-801

Task Description

3. Clock Installation (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) This task has the steps to install the clock.

B. References

Reference Title 20-40-12-400-802 ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation (P/B 201) 31-25-00-710-803 Clocks - System Test (P/B 501) 31-25-00-710-804 Clocks - System Test (P/B 501)

C. Expendables/Parts

D. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

E. Clock Installation

SUBTASK 31-25-11-860-003 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK 31-25-11 SUBTASK 31-25-11-440-001 CAUTION DO NOT TOUCH THE CONNECTOR PINS, OR OTHER CONDUCTORS. IF YOU TOUCH THESE CONDUCTORS, ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE COMPONENTS. (2) Before the clock [1] is touched, do this task: ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Installation, TASK 20-40-12-400-802. SUBTASK 31-25-11-420-001 (3) Install the clock [1]: (a) Remove the protective covers from the electrical connector. (b) Examine the electrical connector for bent or broken pins, dirt, and damage. (c) Connect the electrical connector. (d) Put the clock [1] into the instrument panel. (e) Tighten the screws [2] and screws [3] that hold the clock [1] to the mounting clamp. NOTE: The large screws hold the clock to the mounting clamp. The small screws hold the mounting clamp to the instrument panel. F. Installation Test SUBTASK 31-25-11-700-001 (1) Do the installation test: NOTE: For the GLU-925 and GLU-2100 during User Initiated Self-Test units will output SSM status of FT (Functional Test) and stop output GNSS data such as Date and Time until the Self test is complete. User Initiated Self Test can be up to 30 seconds in duration. (a) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name A 1 C00736 MISC CLOCK DISPLAY A 2 C00737 MISC CLOCK (b) Set the clock [1] to the correct date and Greenwich Mean Time (GMT) (TASK 31-25-00-710-803 or TASK 31-25-00-710-804). (c) Let the clock [1] operate for not less than two minutes. (d) Make sure that the clock [1] shows the correct time.

31-31-00-040-801Flight Data Recorder System - Deactivation ATA 31 · Task 040-801

Task Description

2. Flight Data Recorder System - Deactivation

A. General

(1) This procedure removes electrical power to the Flight Data Recorder System (FDRS).

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right 240 Subzone - Passenger Compartment - Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 1016.00

C. Flight Data Recorder System Deactivation

SUBTASK 31-31-00-860-739 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name C 9 C00109 FLIGHT RECORDER AC C 10 C00468 FLIGHT RECORDER DC

D. Flight Data Recorder System - Tryout

NOTE: This tryout is to make sure that the FDRS in a zero energy state. SUBTASK 31-31-00-860-740 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name C 9 C00109 FLIGHT RECORDER AC C 10 C00468 FLIGHT RECORDER DC 31-31-00 SUBTASK 31-31-00-710-051 (2) Put the FLIGHT RECORDER TEST switch, on the P5 aft overhead panel, to the TEST position. (a) Make sure that the flight recorder OFF light is on. (b) Make sure that all of the fault lights, on the Flight Data Acquisition Unit (FDAU) front panel, do not come on. SUBTASK 31-31-00-710-052 (3) Put the FLIGHT RECORDER TEST switch, on the P5 aft overhead panel, to the NORM position. (a) Push one of the MASTER CAUTION lights on the P7 glareshield panel. 1) Make sure that the two MASTER CAUTION lights are off. 2) Make sure that the flight recorder OFF light is on.

31-31-00-700-801Flight Data Recorder - Single State Analog Discrete Parameter Test ATA 31 · Task 700-801

Task Description

4. Flight Data Recorder - Single State Analog Discrete Parameter Test NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) This task includes the steps to set parameters recorded by the Flight Data Recorder (FDR) before the data is downloaded. (2) Depending on the tool being used, only one T ask Card needs to be completed for each schedule per the airplane. 31-31-00

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201) 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201) 29-11-00-860-805 Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal (P/B 201) 29-21-00-000-802 Standby Hydraulic System Power Removal (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 212 Flight Compartment - Right 240 Subzone - Passenger Compartment - Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 1016.00

D. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

E. Prepare for the Task

SUBTASK 31-31-00-700-010 (1) Put the FLIGHT RECORDER TEST switch, on the P5 aft overhead panel, to the TEST position. F. Flight Data Recorder - Single State Analog Discrete Parameter Test SUBTASK 31-31-00-700-008 (1) Do these steps to set the applicable overheat/fire discrete signals: (a) Push the fire test switch, on the P8 overheat/fire detection panel, to the OVHT/FIRE position and hold for at least 4 seconds. 1) Make sure that these lights, on the P8 overheat/fire detection panel, are on: a) ENG 1 fire handle light b) ENG 2 fire handle light c) Wheel Well light d) APU fire light. SUBTASK 31-31-00-700-012 (2) Push and hold the cargo smoke detection and fire suppression test button, on the P8-75 panel, for at least 4 seconds. (a) Make sure that these lights, on the P8-75 panel, are on: 1) EXT FWD and EXT AFT green lights come on. 2) FWD and AFT red switchlights come on. 3) DISCH amber light comes on. 4) The DETECTOR FAULT light is off. (b) Make sure that these lights, on the P7 pilot's lightshield, are on: 1) The Captain's and First Officer's FIRE WARN red lights come on. (c) Make sure that the aural warning unit gives the bell sound. SUBTASK 31-31-00-700-013 (3) Do these steps to set the stall warning discrete signal: (a) Push the NO. 1 STALL WARNING TEST switch. 31-31-00 1) Make sure that the captains shaker operates. (b) Push the NO. 2 STALL WARNING TEST switch. 1) Make sure that the first officers shaker operates. SUBTASK 31-31-00-700-014 (4) Do these steps to set the applicable hydraulic system low pressure discrete signals: (a) Make sure that the A and B hydraulic systems are not pressurized. 1) To remove hydraulic pressure, do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805. (b) Make sure that the STBY hydraulic system is not pressurized. 1) To remove hydraulic pressure, do this task: Standby Hydraulic System Power Removal, TASK 29-21-00-000-802. (c) Do these steps to set the low hydraulic pressure system A engine 1 discrete signal: 1) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 2) Remove connector D2684 from the system A engine 1 low pressure switch, S796. 3) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. 4) Make sure that the ENG 1 LOW PRESSURE light of system A, on the P5 hydraulic pump panel, is off. 5) Wait at least 4 seconds. NOTE: The test could take up to a few minutes. 6) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 7) Install connector D2684 to the system A engine 1 low pressure switch, S796. 8) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. 9) Make sure that the ENG 1 LOW PRESSURE light of system A, on the P5 hydraulic pump panel, is on. (d) Do these steps to set the low hydraulic pressure system B engine 2 discrete signal: 1) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 2) Remove connector D2686 from the system B engine 2 low pressure switch, S797. 3) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. 4) Make sure that the ENG 2 LOW PRESSURE light of system B, on the P5 hydraulic pump panel, is off. 5) Wait at least 4 seconds. NOTE: The test could take up to a few minutes. 6) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. 7) Install connector D2686 to the system B engine 2 low pressure switch, S797. 8) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. 9) Make sure that the ENG 2 LOW PRESSURE light of system B, on the P5 hydraulic pump panel, is on. (e) Do these steps to set the standby hydraulic pressure transmitter discrete signal: 31-31-00 WARNING MAKE SURE THAT PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF ALL CONTROL SURFACES BEFORE YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. AILERONS, RUDDERS, ELEVATORS, FLAPS, SPOILERS, LANDING GEAR, AND THRUST REVERSERS CAN MOVE QUICKLY WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT 1) Make sure that the FLT CONTROL hydraulic pressure system switches A and B, on the P5 panel, are not in the STBY RUD position. 2) Set the FLT CONTROL switch A, on the P5 panel, to the STBY RUD position. a) Make sure the STANDBY HYD low pressure light, on the P5 panel, is off. NOTE: The STANDBY HYD low pressure light may come on briefly when the FLT CONTROL switch is set to STBY RUD. 3) To get access to P92 panel, open this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door WARNING WHEN YOU OPEN THE P91 AND P92 PANELS, MAKE SURE THAT THE OUTER DOOR STAYS AS OPEN AS POSSIBLE. IF THE OUTER DOOR TURNS IN, THE ATTACHED DOOR COMPONENTS COULD TOUCH THE INNER DOOR COMPONENTS. THIS CAN CAUSE AN ARC CONDITION WHEN YOU SUPPLY POWER. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT AND INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR. WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE CONDUCTORS IN THE P91 AND P92 PANELS. BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU GET ACCESS TO THE CIRCUIT BREAKERS ON THE INNER SIDE OF THE P91 AND P92 PANELS (ROW F). IF IT IS POSSIBLE, REMOVE AIRPLANE ELECTRICAL POWER FIRST . THE P91 AND P92 PANELS HAVE HIGH VOLTAGES AND CURRENTS. ELECTRICAL VOLTAGE AND CURRENT CAN KILL YOU OR CAUSE INJURIES. 4) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: Power Distribution Panel Number 2, P92 Row Col Number Name F 2 C01449 STANDBY HYDRAULIC PUMP a) Make sure that the STANDBY HYD low pressure light, on the P5 panel, is on. 5) Wait at least 4 seconds. NOTE: The test could take up to a few minutes. 6) Set the FLT CONTROL switch A, on the P5 panel, to the ON position. 31-31-00 WARNING WHEN YOU OPEN THE P91 AND P92 PANELS, MAKE SURE THAT THE OUTER DOOR STAYS AS OPEN AS POSSIBLE. IF THE OUTER DOOR TURNS IN, THE ATTACHED DOOR COMPONENTS COULD TOUCH THE INNER DOOR COMPONENTS. THIS CAN CAUSE AN ARC CONDITION WHEN YOU SUPPLY POWER. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT AND INJURY TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR. WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE CONDUCTORS IN THE P91 AND P92 PANELS. BE CAREFUL WHEN YOU GET ACCESS TO THE CIRCUIT BREAKERS ON THE INNER SIDE OF THE P91 AND P92 PANELS (ROW F). IF IT IS POSSIBLE, REMOVE AIRPLANE ELECTRICAL POWER FIRST . THE P91 AND P92 PANELS HAVE HIGH VOLTAGES AND CURRENTS. ELECTRICAL VOLTAGE AND CURRENT CAN KILL YOU OR CAUSE INJURIES. 7) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker: Power Distribution Panel Number 2, P92 Row Col Number Name F 2 C01449 STANDBY HYDRAULIC PUMP SUBTASK 31-31-00-700-015 (5) Do these steps to set the cabin altitude > 10K discrete signal: (a) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. (b) Remove connector D776 from the cabin pressure switch (S128). (c) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. (d) Apply a ground to pin A of D776 connector. 1) Make sure that the aural warning horn in the flight compartment comes on. (e) Wait at least 4 seconds. (f) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812. (g) Remove the ground from pin A of D776 connector. (h) Install connector D776 to the cabin pressure switch (S128).

G. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 31-31-00-700-009 (1) Put the FLIGHT RECORDER TEST switch, on the P5 aft overhead panel, in the NORMAL position. SUBTASK 31-31-00-410-010 (2) Close this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

31-31-00-970-803Copy the Data from the Solid State Flight Data Recorder with a Hand Held Download Unit ATA 31 · Task 970-803

Task Description

5. Copy the Data from the Solid State Flight Data Recorder with a Hand Held Download Unit Figure 31-31-11-990-801 NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) This task uses a handheld download unit, COM-913 (HHDLU) to make a copy of the flight data from a Solid-State Flight Data Recorder (SSFDR) while the Flight Data Recorder (FDR) is in the airplane. (2) The copied data then can be analyzed at a different location by the applicable airline personnel.

B. References

Reference Title 25-21-45-000-801 Ceiling Panel - Removal (P/B 401) 25-21-45-000-803-001 Main Ceiling Panel - Removal (P/B 401) 25-21-45-400-801 Ceiling Panel - Installation (P/B 401) 25-21-45-400-803-001 Main Ceiling Panel - Installation (P/B 401) 31-31-11-990-801 Figure: Flight Data Recorder Installation (P/B 401)

C. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-913 Download Unit - Handheld Part #: 69001074-090 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 69001074-001 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 69001074-060 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 69001074-070 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 69001074-080 Supplier: 97896 Opt Part #: 964-0446-001 Supplier: 97896 STD-1048 Stepladder - 6 foot (1.83m)

D. Location Zones

Zone Area 212 Flight Compartment - Right 240 Subzone - Passenger Compartment - Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 1016.00

E. Copy the Data from the Solid State Flight Data Recorder with a Hand Held Download Unit

NOTE: After each file is erased, the HHDLU makes sure that the HHDLU has sufficient memory space. If there is sufficient memory, the HHDLU will start to make a copy. 31-31-00-730-801Flight Data Recorder System - System Test ATA 31 · Task 730-801

Task Description

3. Flight Data Recorder System - System Test

A. General

(1) The system test has procedures to do a check of the inputs to the Flight Data Recorder System (FDRS). You can do the tests out of sequence and you can do each test independently. (2) The tests in this procedure are written to the standard Boeing application software. If customized software is installed, part of the test may not look the same. Installation of the Boeing application software may be necessary.

B. References

ATA 31 · 31-31-09

14 tasks
31-31-09-000-801Underwater Locator Beacon Removal ATA 31 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Underwater Locator Beacon Removal (Figure 201) NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) The Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) has a battery as the power source. The ULB has no external electrical connections.

B. References

Reference Title 31-31-11-000-802 Flight Data Recorder Removal (P/B 401)

C. Tools/Equipment

Reference Description STD-1048 Stepladder - 6 foot (1.83m)

D. Location Zones

Zone Area 140 Subzone - Body Station 727.00 to Body Station 1016.00 240 Subzone - Passenger Compartment - Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 1016.00

E. Underwater Locator Beacon Removal

SUBTASK 31-31-09-010-002 (1) To get access to the flight data recorder [2] in the aft passenger compartment, open the lowered ceiling panel [9]. D. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 31-31-09-860-001 (1) Measure the battery voltage of the ELP-362D ULB [4]. Use a high-impedance digital voltmeter with a minimum input impedance of 10 Megohms. (a) Put the negative meter lead on the water switch. 31-31-09 RYR ALL; FLIGHT DATA RECORDERS WITH DATASONIC ULBS (Continued) (b) Put the positive meter lead on the bare aluminum surface of the beacon housing. (c) Read the voltmeter.

E. Removal Procedure

SUBTASK 31-31-09-800-002 (1) If the measured voltage is less than 6.0 Volts, send the ELP-362D ULB [4] to the manufacturer for servicing. SUBTASK 31-31-09-020-006 (2) If the measured voltage is 6.0 Volts or more, remove the battery, G50272 [28] from the ELP-362D ULB [4]: CAUTION DO NOT HOLD THE ULB WITH A VISE. THIS CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE ULB. (a) Hold the ULB [4] body with a radiator hose - 1-1/4 Inch Diameter, 5 Inch Length, STD-1066. (b) Use the underwater locator beacon torque adapter, COM-2543 to remove the end-cap [25] identified as “BATTERY ACCESS”. (c) Turn the housing up to remove the battery from the unit. (d) Discard the battery [28]. NOTE: Refer to local instructions when you discard the battery [28]. F. Installation Procedure SUBTASK 31-31-09-420-002 (1) Install the ULB battery, G50272 [28]: (a) Set the battery [28] until the arrow points to the top end of the unit. NOTE: The battery label has an arrow mark. (b) On the date label [23], write the next scheduled replacement date for the new ULB battery that you installed. NOTE: The date label [23] is blank so you can write in a replacement date based on your maintenance schedule. CAUTION REMOVE ALL OF THE CONTAMINATION FROM THE THREADS AND THE O-RING GROOVES. CONTAMINATION CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE THREADS. THREAD DAMAGE CAN CAUSE LEAKS. (c) Clean the threads and the O-ring contact area in the ULB [4] body. CAUTION INSTALL THE ULB BATTERY [28] CORRECTLY . INCORRECT POLARITY WILL CAUSE PERMANENT DAMAGE TO THE ULB. (d) Put the new battery, G50272 [28] in the ULB [4] with the end identified INSERT THIS END in first. (e) Do these steps to test the beacon off-current: 31-31-09 RYR ALL; FLIGHT DATA RECORDERS WITH DATASONIC ULBS (Continued) 1) Put the positive probe of a digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793 on the positive end of the battery [28]. NOTE: Use Multimeter Part #:289 Supplier :55026 for measuring current below 3 microamperes. 2) Put the negative probe on the outer surface of the ULB [4]. 3) Make sure that the multimeter shows an electrical current of 3 microamperes or less. 4) If the current is more than 3 microamperes, then replace the ULB [4]. (f) Remove and discard the O-ring [26] from its groove in the end-cap [25]. CAUTION REMOVE ALL OF THE CONTAMINATION FROM THE THREADS AND THE O-RING GROOVES. CONTAMINATION CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE THREADS. THREAD DAMAGE CAN CAUSE LEAKS. (g) Clean the threads and the O-ring groove in the end cap. NOTE: Lubricant and O-ring are supplied in the battery replacement kit. (h) Apply a thin layer of lubricant, D50082 to the new O-ring, G50273 [26], O-ring groove, and threads. (i) Put the lubricated O-ring, G50273 [26] in the end-cap groove. (j) Attach the end-cap [25] to the housing. (k) Use the underwater locator beacon torque adapter, COM-2543 to install the end-cap [25] tightly. NOTE: Only use hand force on the underwater locator beacon torque adapter, COM-2543. (l) Torque the end-cap [25] to 25 to 30 inch pounds.

31-31-09-700-805Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a 42A12 Test Set ATA 31 · Task 700-805

Task Description

6. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a 42A12 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-10768 Test Set - 42A12, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Opt Part #: 42A12-1 Supplier: 94970

B. Procedure

SUBTASK 31-31-09-700-007 (1) If you have a 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768, do this test of the ULB: NOTE: 42A12 can do a test for all ULBs. (a) Put the 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768 as close as possible to the ULB. (b) Set the GAIN control switch on the 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768 to the maximum clockwise position. NOTE: A background noise is heard. If you do not hear noise from the test set, replace the test set battery. (c) Set the TUNING control switch to the middle position. (d) Make sure that the 42A12 ULB Test Set, COM-10768 operates correctly. 1) Rub your thumb and fingers together in front of the microphone to make sure that it operates. NOTE: This will produce a rushing noise from the speaker. a) Make sure that you hear sounds through the speaker. (e) Use tape to attach a piece of wire, a shorting tab, or other conductive material to the ULB case and to the center of the water switch. NOTE: This will make a short circuit from the center of the water switch to the outer part of the ULB. (f) Set the GAIN control switch to a comfortable listening level. (g) Point the microphone of the test set towards the water switch end of the beacon for best results. 1) Make sure you hear a pulse tone. (h) Remove the wire, shorting tab, or other conducting material from the ULB case and the center of the water switch. (i) Set the GAIN control switch to the OFF position. (j) Make sure that the water switch on the ULB has no grease or dirt. (k) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

31-31-09-700-804Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS200 Test Set ATA 31 · Task 700-804

Task Description

11. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS200 Test Set

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-10770 Test Set - TS200, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Opt Part #: TS200 Supplier: 94970

B. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS200 Test Set

SUBTASK 31-31-09-720-008 (1) If you have a TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770, do this test of the Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB): NOTE: TS200 can do a test for all Dukane ULBs. (b) Put the tip of the probe on the silver pad of the water switch at the end of the beacon. 1) The LCD display will show the battery voltage of the beacon. (c) Refer to the applicable battery code for the minimum permitted range of the beacon battery voltage: NOTE: Examine the battery replacement label to find the battery code. 1) Code A – 3.55 Volts 2) Code B – 2.97 Volts 3) Code C – 2.97 Volts 4) Code D – 2.97 Volts 5) Code F – 2.97 Volts RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH UNDERWATER LOCATOR BEACON PART NUMBER 266-E5542-00 6) Without battery code – 2.97 Volts. RYR ALL (d) Push the red button on the TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770. 1) The beacon starts and you hear a pinging noise from the TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770. (e) Remove the test probe clip of the TS200 ULB test set, COM-10770. (f) If it is necessary, replace the ULB. (g) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB has no grease or dirt. (h) If it is necessary, do the steps that follow: 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 31-31-09 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

31-31-09-700-807L-3 Underwater Locator Beacon Off-Current Test ATA 31 · Task 700-807

Task Description

12. L-3 Underwater Locator Beacon Off-Current Test (Figure 206)

A. General

(1) This test should be performed on a new battery every 6-years, immediately prior to installing in the beacon to ensure maximum power.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-1793 Multimeter - Digital/Analog (or equivalent meter meets task requirements) Part #: 117 Supplier: 89536 Part #: 260-8XPI Supplier: 55026 Part #: 287 Supplier: 89536 Part #: 289 Supplier: 89536 Part #: 87V Supplier: 89536 Part #: FLUKE 27 II Supplier: 89536 Part #: FLUKE-77-4 Supplier: 89536 Part #: WA2025 Supplier: 4MJL8 Opt Part #: 187 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 189 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 21 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 27 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 77 SERIES III Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: 87 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: FLUKE 27 Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: MODEL 27 Supplier: 89536

C. Procedure

SUBTASK 31-31-09-700-008 CAUTION BEACONS WITH GREATER THAN 3−MICROAMPERES OFF CURRENT SHOULD BE TAKEN OUT OF SERVICE IMMEDIATELY . (1) Do these steps to test the L-3 ULB [4] off-current: (a) Put the positive probe of the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793 on the positive end of the battery [28]. (b) Put the negative probe on the outer surface of the L-3 ULB [4]. (c) Make sure that the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793 shows an electrical current of 3 microamperes or less. 1) If the current is more than 3 microamperes, replace the L-3 ULB [4] (L-3 Underwater Locator Beacon Battery - Replacement, TASK 31-31-09-960-805).

31-31-09-700-808L-3 Underwater Locator Beacon Operational Test with 17TES0015 Test Set ATA 31 · Task 700-808

Task Description

13. L-3 Underwater Locator Beacon Operational Test with 17TES0015 Test Set

A. General

(1) The L-3AR underwater locator beacon test set, COM-15070 is a battery operated heterodyne type receiver that operates at an ultrasonic frequency of 37.5 kHz. (2) The Test Set is a self-contained, hand held functional tester for the L-3AR Underwater Locator Beacon. (3) Use the internal microphone and loudspeaker to perform an operational test on a beacon without removing it from the recorder’s Cabin System Management Unit (CSMU) mount.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-15070 Test Set - 17TES0015, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Part #: 17TES0015 Supplier: 06141

C. Procedure

SUBTASK 31-31-09-700-010 (1) Test the beacon battery voltage: NOTE: This test should be performed after the beacon battery has been installed on the recorders CSMU. It verifies the proper operation of the beacon after mounting on the recorder’s CSMU. (a) Turn the underwater locator beacon test set, COM-15070 ON using the small slide switch located on the left side of the housing. (b) If the display reads LB, the test set battery voltage has fallen below the level required for operation, and the 9 Volt battery should be replaced. (c) To test the beacon battery voltage, attach the test probe clip to the beacon case. The clip has a spring action that will allow it to slip over the housing on the beacon. (d) Put the tip of the probe in contact with the metal pad at the center of the water switch. (e) Read the battery voltage from the test set display. 1) If the battery voltage is below 2.97 Volts, replace the beacon (L-3 Underwater Locator Beacon Battery - Replacement, TASK 31-31-09-960-805). SUBTASK 31-31-09-700-011 (2) Test the beacon operation: NOTE: This test should be performed after the beacon is installed on the recorder’s CSMU. It verifies the proper operation of the beacon after mounting on the CSMU. (a) Attach the test probe clip to the beacon case. The clip has a spring action that will allow it to slip over the housing on the beacon. (b) Place the tip of the probe in contact with the metal pad at the center of the water switch. (c) Press and hold the red button on the test set. NOTE: The test set display will be blank during this operation. 31-31-09 (d) If the beacon is operating properly, a pinging sound will be audible from the test set speakers. NOTE: High levels of background noise can interfere with this test. (e) If difficulty is experienced, this test should be repeated in a quieter environment. (f) Turn off the underwater locator beacon test set, COM-15070, and remove the beacon from the test probe clip. (g) If required, record the beacon’s serial number and expiration date on the appropriate recorder data record.

31-31-09-700-809Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS500 Test Set ATA 31 · Task 700-809

Task Description

15. Underwater Locator Beacon Test with a TS500 Test Set

A. General

(1) This procedure has steps to do a test of the Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) with a TS500 Test Set. NOTE: TS500 can do a test for all ULBs.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-19623 Test Set - TS500, Underwater Locator Beacon (ULB) Part #: TS500 Supplier: 94970

C. Procedure

SUBTASK 31-31-09-700-012 (1) Testing for the ULB battery voltage: (a) Attach the test probe clip of the TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623 to the beacon in its mount. (b) Put the tip of the probe on the silver pad of the water switch at the end of the beacon. 1) The LCD display will show the battery voltage of the beacon. (c) Refer to the applicable battery code for the minimum permitted range of the beacon battery voltage: NOTE: Examine the battery replacement label to find the battery code. 1) Code F - 2.97 Volts (2) Testing for the operation of the ULB: (a) Keep the probes connected to the ULB. NOTE: The test set display will remain blank during this test. (b) Push the red button on the TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623. 1) The beacon starts and you hear a pinging noise from the TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623. 2) The TS500 underwater locator beacon test set, COM-19623 LED indicator will light up red every time the ULB pings. (c) Remove the test probe clip of the TS500. (3) If voltage or operation test does not pass, replace the ULB if necessary. (a) Make sure that the water switch end of the ULB has no grease or dirt. (b) If necessary, do the steps that follow: 31-31-09 1) Clean the switch with water and detergent. 2) Dry the switch with a clean cloth.

31-31-22-470-801Flight Data Acquisition Unit (FDAU) Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader ATA 31 · Task 470-801

Task Description

2. Flight Data Acquisition Unit (FDAU) Software Installation with a Portable Data Loader

A. General

(1) This task gives the instruction to install the software into the Flight Data Acquisition Unit (FDAU). (a) The FDAU must contain these pieces of software: 1) Mandatory FDAU software 2) Airplane Condition And Monitoring System (ACMS) non-mandatory FDAU software. (2) A Portable Data Loader (PDL) and Control Display Unit (CDU) are necessary for this task. A data loader control panel and PDL interface connector are also necessary. The data loader control panel is installed above the DATA TRANSFER UNIT RECEPTACLE connector, on the P61 data loader control panel. (3) A PDL is not a Boeing supplied part. Refer to the PDL supplier for instructions for operations. PDLs have a disk drive for software installation from disks. Some PDLs have an internal mass storage device. If the software is stored in the PDL, then disks are not necessary. (4) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can install software. 31-31-22 (5) The word "media" defines a data storage device. Examples of media are CDs, mass storage devices, diskettes, or floppy disks. (6) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders. (7) Sequence for the Digital Flight Data Acquisition Unit (DFDAU) software installation is following: (a) Mandatory DFDAU software (mandatory load) (b) Broadcast DFDAU software (mandatory load) (c) ACMS and application software (non-mandatory load) (d) ACMS database (non-mandatory load).

B. References

Reference Title 20-15-11-400-801 On-Airplane Software - Installation (P/B 201)

C. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. 31-31-22-470-803ACMS Data Output from the FDAU with the use of the Portable Data Loader ATA 31 · Task 470-803

Task Description

3. ACMS Data Output from the FDAU with the use of the Portable Data Loader

A. General

(1) This procedure tells you how to download ACMS data from the flight data acquisition unit (FDAU) with the use of the PDL. (2) A portable data loader (PDL) and a control display unit (CDU) are necessary for this procedure. (3) The airplane must be on the ground with the engines shutdown before you can download ACMS data. (4) The word "media" defines a data storage device. Examples of media are CDs, mass storage devices, diskettes, or floppy disks. (5) You must format the media with your applicable download instructions before you can make a copy of the ACMS data. Please refer to your FDAU vendor's manual on how to format the recording media. (6) Modern software loaders that meet the security requirements defined in ARINC 645-1 or newer are recommended to use. The procedures for modern software loaders will be marked as preferred for now and will be the only procedures available after removing floppy disk loaders procedures in the future. Please check with your Software Loader Supplier for current capabilities since this is still maturing across current and future software loaders.

B. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. (b) Do these steps on the FDAU to monitor the loading process: 1) Push ESC key until TELEDYNE CNTRLS show on the FDAU display. 2) Push ENT key to show MANDATORY MENU. 3) Push (+) to show NON-MANDATORY MENU. 4) Push ENT to show VERSION. 5) Push (+) two times to show LOAD STATUS. 6) Push ENT . NOTE: After the load is completed, the disk will automatically eject and the FDAU will start the execution of the new application. SUBTASK 31-31-22-020-021 (3) Remove the software upload disk. (a) If necessary, install the disk removed before the installation of the software upload disk. 31-31-22-970-801DFDAU Input Monitoring Maintenance Practices with DITS or Mnemonics ATA 31 · Task 970-801

Task Description

8. DFDAU Input Monitoring Maintenance Practices with DITS or Mnemonics

A. General

(1) A task to use the Digital Flight Data Acquisition Unit (DFDAU) for input monitoring with Digital Information Transfer System (DITS) or Mnemonics. (2) For DITS the labels, Source Destination Identifier (SDI) and bit definitions are contained in the Interconnect document for that Line Replaceable Unit (LRU). The label and bit definitions are necessary for this task. If necessary, speak to your engineering department for specific information.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Procedure for Digital Information Transfer System (DITS)

SUBTASK 31-31-22-970-011 (1) Do these steps on the Control Display Unit (CDU) for input monitoring. (a) Select the MENU key on the CDU. (b) Select the <ACMS line select key. (c) Select the <DISPLAY DATA line select key. (d) Select the <DISP line select key adjacent to DITS. NOTE: XXX is the DFDAU port number. YYY is the label for the bus. ZZ is the SDI (01 is sys 1, 10 is sys 2, 11 is sys 3 or center). F is the Format of the data, (B is Binary, H is Hex). Example 066/271/01/B. This example is to request data from port 66, label 271, SDI 01, Binary format. (e) Put the information in the scratchpad in this format XXX/YYY/ZZ/F . (f) Push an active line select key. NOTE: The data can show in real time, or you can PRINT> line select key.

D. Procedures for Mnemonics

SUBTASK 31-31-22-970-012 (1) Do these steps on the CDU to monitor inputs. (a) Select the MENU key on the CDU. (b) Select the <ACMS line select key. (c) Select the <DISPLAY DATA line select key. (d) Select the <DISP line select key adjacent to MNEMONIC. 31-31-22 (e) Put the MNEMONIC name into the scratchpad and push an active line select key. NOTE: The data will then be displayed in engineering units such as knots or PSI. For example if you enter TAT .L for the Mnemonic, total air temperature will show in degrees. NOTE: The Mnemonics are programmed in the ACMS database and can change based on customization of the database.

(j) Do these steps to test a right aileron position transmitter [9] installation: 1) Rotate the Captain's control wheel to the full left position. 2) Make sure that you get an octal value between 7167 - 7311. 3) Rotate the Captain's control wheel to the full right position. 4) Make sure that you get an octal value between 0463 - 0605. (k) Do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805. Prepare for the Control Wheel Position Sensor Installation SUBTASK 31-31-32-860-002 (1) Make sure that this circuit breaker is open and has safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name C 8 C00544 FLIGHT RECORDER POSITION SENSOR SUBTASK 31-31-32-860-003 (2) If the hydraulic system are not depressurized, do this task: Pressure from the Aileron Hydraulic Systems A and B - Deactivation, TASK 27-11-00-860-801.

G. Procedure

SUBTASK 31-31-32-020-005 WARNING PRIOR AND DURING REMOVAL/INSTALLATION PROCEDURES OF THE SURFACE POSITION SENSORS, DO NOT ATTEMPT TO OPERATE ANY FLIGHT CONTROL SYSTEM OR DAMAGE TO CONTROL MECHANISMS AND INJURY TO PERSONNEL MAY RESULT . (1) Install the control wheel position sensor [5]: 31-31-32 WARNING USE NITRILE GLOVES FOR SKIN PROTECTION WHEN YOU USE COR- BAN 27L. IF IT GETS ON YOUR SKIN, IMMEDIATELY REMOVE IT WITH WATER. IF THIS MATERIAL GETS IN YOUR EYES, IMMEDIATELY FLUSH YOUR EYES WITH WATER. GET MEDICAL AID. THIS MATERIAL CONTAINS FLAMMABLE AGENTS WHICH CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL. (a) Apply Cor-Ban 27L Compound, G50237 (preferred) or corrosion inhibiting material, G50136 (alternate) to the shaft of the sensor. NOTE: Make sure the compound does not get on the sensor bearing. This can cause friction on the bearing. (b) Install the rig pins A/S-1 and A/S-1A from the test set rigging pin kit, SPL-1585 into the aileron transfer mechanism. 1) Make sure the rig pin A/S-1 is fully in the aileron transfer mechanism. 2) Hold the two rig pins to make sure they do not fall out. (c) Make a pencil line on the sensor mounting bracket so that the line is center and perpendicular to the flat side of the flex coupling hole. NOTE: This will help to align the index mark on the sensor body to the center line of the sensor shaft. (d) Insert the shaft of the sensor into the hole of the flex coupling. (e) Install the mounting bolts [2] and washers [3]. (f) Thread the mounting bolts [2] until it clamp down on the sensor body. (g) Align the index mark on the sensor body to the pencil line on the sensor mounting bracket. (h) Tighten the 3 sensor mounting bolts [2] to 25 - 30 pound-inches (2.8 - 3.4 newton-meters). (i) Tighten the flex coupling screw [4] to 12 - 15 pound-inches (1.4 - 1.7 newton-meters). (j) Install the cable and the cable clamp screws [6]. (k) Install the sensor [5]. (l) Install the electrical connector [1].

SUBTASK 31-31-41-820-001 (7) Do these steps to adjust the rudder position sensor: (a) Do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805. (b) Loosen the screw [3] on the transmitter support brackets. 31-31-41 (c) Set the tester to subframe 0, word 37. (d) Turn the body of the rudder position transmitter slowly in each direction until you get an octal value between 7745 to 0034 on the tester. (e) Tighten the transmitter support bracket screw [3] to 30 in-lb (3.4 N·m) - 35 in-lb (4.0 N·m). (f) Do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Pressurization, TASK 29-11-00-860-801. (g) Put the rudder to the full left position. 1) Make sure that the tester shows 7032 to 7121. (h) Put the rudder to the full right position. 1) Make sure that the tester shows 0656 to 0745. (i) Do this task: Put the Rudder Hydraulic systems A, B, and Standby Back to the Condition Before the Pressurization, TASK 27-21-00-840-801. (j) Do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805. SUBTASK 31-31-41-410-001 (8) Close this access panel: Number Name/Location 324HL Vertical Fin, Access F. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition SUBTASK 31-31-41-860-008 (1) Put the FLIGHT RECORDER TEST switch, on the P5 aft overhead panel, to the NORM position. SUBTASK 31-31-41-840-004 (2) For the portable tester, do these steps: (a) Do this task: Flight Data Recorder System - Deactivation, TASK 31-31-00-040-801. (b) Remove power from the tester. (c) Remove the tester from the Flight Data Recorder System (FDRS). (d) Do this task: Flight Data Recorder System - Activation, TASK 31-31-00-440-801. (e) If it is necessary, close access to the FDR (TASK 31-31-11-400-802). (f) If it is removed, install the shorting plug on the system test plug on the P18 panel.

C. Consumable Materials

D. Expendables/Parts

E. Location Zones

Zone Area 112 Area Forward of Nose Landing Gear Wheel Well F. Access Panels Number Name/Location 112A Forward Access Door

G. Installation Procedure

SUBTASK 31-31-42-860-002 (1) Make sure that this circuit breaker is open and has safety tag: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name C 8 C00544 FLIGHT RECORDER POSITION SENSOR SUBTASK 31-31-42-860-003 (2) If the rudder hydraulic systems are not depressurized, do this task: Pressure from the Rudder Hydraulic Systems A, B, and Standby - Deactivation, TASK 27-21-00-800-802. 31-31-42 SUBTASK 31-31-42-020-002 WARNING PRIOR AND DURING REMOVAL/INSTALLATION PROCEDURES OF THE SURFACE POSITION SENSORS, DO NOT ATTEMPT TO OPERATE ANY FLIGHT CONTROL SYSTEM OR DAMAGE TO CONTROL MECHANISMS AND INJURY TO PERSONNEL MAY RESULT . (3) Install the rudder pedal position sensor [2]: (a) Set the rudder pedals to the neutral position. (b) Install R2 rigging pin kit, SPL-1585, into the first officer rudder pedal. (c) Turn the sensor until the mark on the shaft of the sensor aligns with the EZ mark on the sensor body. (d) Install the rudder pedal position sensor [2] into the sensor bracket [5]. (e) Apply a thin layer of compound, C00174, on the internal and external surfaces of the thread on the sensor clamp screws [3]. (f) Install the sensor clamp screws [3], washers [11]. (g) Tighten the screws [3] to 30-35 pound-inches (3-4 newton-meters). (h) Install the cable drum [6] to the sensor shaft. (i) Install screw [4], washer [12] and nut [13]. (j) Tighten the screw [4] to 30-35 pound-inches (3-4 newton-meters). (k) Do these steps to install the cable C: 1) Install cable C between the top part of the quadrant [8] and the top spool on the cable drum [6]. 2) Install the cotter pins [9] to the top part of quadrant [8]. 3) Install cotter pin [10] to the top spool on the cable drum [6]. (l) Do these steps to install cable A and B: 1) Install the ball terminal of cable A and the cotter pin [9] to the lower part of the quadrant [8]. CAUTION MAKE SURE YOU INSTALL COTTER PIN [9] CORRECTLY . THE HEAD OF THE COTTER PIN SHOULD BE VISIBLE ON THE LOWER, OUTER SURFACE OF THE CABLE DRUM AS SHOWN IN FIGURE 401, SHEET 3. IF THE COTTER PIN IS NOT INSTALL CORRECTLY , DAMAGE TO THE RUDDER PEDAL POSITION SENSOR CABLE CAN OCCUR. 2) Install the ball terminal cable B and the cotter pin [9] to the lower spool of the cable drum [6]. 3) Install the spring [8] between the loose ends of cable A and B. 4) Lubricate the cables with grease, D00016. 5) Make sure the dimension across the spring [7] between the cable terminal is 2.03 ± 0.02 inches (51.56 ± 0.51 mm). 6) Tighten the two sensor clamp screws [3] to 30-35 pound-inches (3-4 newton-meters). (m) Install the electrical connector [1] onto the rudder pedal position sensor [2]. 31-31-42 SUBTASK 31-31-42-410-001 (4) If you do not accomplish the installation test immediately, close this access panel: Number Name/Location 112A Forward Access Door SUBTASK 31-31-42-410-003 (5) Do this task: Rudder Pedal Position Sensor Installation Test, TASK 31-31-42-700-801.

Installation Test SUBTASK 31-31-51-980-001 (1) Do these steps to set the B dimension (Figure 402): NOTE: The B dimension measures the distance between the center of the upper and lower Gimbal pins on the stabilizer trim jackscrew. To do this, one person must be in the tail of the airplane with a headset and the other person in the flight compartment. (a) Turn the stab trim wheel handle on the control stand to set the horizontal stabilizer to 4 units of trim. NOTE: This B dimension is equivalent to the horizontal stabilizer at 4 units of trim (zero degree). (b) Use the trammel trammel bar, SPL-1677, to measure the B dimension. 1) Make sure that the B dimension is 39.89 ±0.01 in. (1013.21 ±0.25 mm). SUBTASK 31-31-51-700-001 (5) Do these steps to adjust the elevator position transmitter: (a) Make sure that the elevator trailing edge is positioned in line with index on tailcone within +/- 0.06 in. (1.52 mm). (b) Make sure that the length of the linkage rod from center to center is 7.23 in. (183.64 mm) - 7.27 in. (184.66 mm). (c) Loosen the screw. (d) Set the tester subframe and word: 1) For the left elevator position transmitter, set the tester to subframe 0, word 4. 2) For the right elevator position transmitter, set the tester to subframe 0, word 10. (e) Rotate the elevator position transmitter slowly in each direction until you get an octal value between 7733 to 7777 or 0000 to 0044 on the tester. (f) Tighten the screw. (g) Turn the thumb wheel on the linkage rod slowly in each direction until you get an octal value between 7733 to 7777 or 0000 to 0044 on the tester. 31-31-51 (h) Lockwire the thumb wheel. NOTE: After final adjustments the tester should shows between 7733 to 7777 or 0000 to 0044 octal when stabilizer is set to 39.89 and elevators are faired to neutral within 0.06 in. (1.52 mm) of the index on the tailcone. SUBTASK 31-31-51-700-002 (6) Do these steps to test the elevator position transmitter signal: WARNING MAKE SURE THAT PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF ALL CONTROL SURFACES BEFORE YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. AILERONS, RUDDERS, ELEVATORS, FLAPS, SLATS, SPOILERS, LANDING GEAR, AND THRUST REVERSERS CAN MOVE QUICKLY WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . CAUTION DO NOT OPERATE THE HYDRAULIC SYSTEM IF THERE IS LESS THAN A SUFFICIENT QUANTITY OF FUEL IN THE APPLICABLE TANK. DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (a) Do this task: Elevator Hydraulic System A and B - Pressurization, TASK 27-31-00-800-801. (b) Make sure that the horizontal stabilizer is at 4 units of trim. (c) Set the tester to subframe 0, word 4. (d) Move the control column full aft against stop. 1) Make sure that the tester shows 6710 to 7056. (e) Move the control column full forward against stop. 1) Make sure that the tester shows 0500 to 0646. (f) Set the tester to subframe 0, word 10. (g) Move the control column full aft against stop. 1) Make sure that the tester shows 0730 to 1056. (h) Move the control column full forward against stop. 1) Make sure that the tester shows 7140 to 7266. (i) Do this task: Remove Pressure from the Elevator Hydraulic Systems A and B, TASK 27-31-00-800-802.

E. Brake Pressure Transmitter Installation Test

SUBTASK 31-31-61-860-009 (1) Do these steps to do an installation test of the alternate brake pressure transmitters, T494 or T495: WARNING MAKE SURE THAT ALL PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF THE CONTROL SURFACES AND LANDING GEAR DOOR AREAS. THE CONTROL SURFACES, THE LANDING GEAR, AND THE LANDING GEAR DOORS CAN MOVE WHEN YOU DO THE AIR MODE SIMULATION. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT THE INBOARD AND OUTBOARD THRUST REVERSER COWLS ARE IN THE CLOSED POSITION BEFORE YOU DO THIS TEST . YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE FLAPS AND COWLS IF THE COWLS ARE OPEN WHEN THE FLAPS EXTEND. CAUTION DO NOT OPERATE THE ELECTRIC MOTOR-DRIVEN PUMP (EMDP) MORE THAN TWO MINUTES UNLESS THE FUEL TANKS CONTAIN MORE THAN 1675 LBS (761 KG) OF FUEL. IF THE PUMP OPERATES FOR TWO MINUTES WITHOUT THE FUEL IN THE TANK, LET THE RESERVOIR GO BACK TO AMBIENT TEMPERATURE BEFORE YOU OPERATE THE PUMP AGAIN. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (a) Supply hydraulic pressure for system A, do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Pressurization, TASK 29-11-00-860-801. 1) Make sure that system B does not have hydraulic pressure. a) If system B has hydraulic pressure, do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal, TASK 29-11-00-860-805. 2) Make sure that the standby hydraulic system does not have hydraulic pressure. a) If the standby hydraulic system has pressure, do this task: Standby Hydraulic System Power Removal, TASK 29-21-00-000-802. (a) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550. RYR ALL SUBTASK 31-31-81-600-001 (6) Manually apply coating, C00064, to the remaining bare aluminium areas on the structure. SUBTASK 31-31-81-600-002 (7) Apply one coat of primer, C00259, to cover all exposed bare areas on the bracket around the bolts [2].

RYR ALL 6) Make sure that DETECTED CABLE field, on the DART screen, indicates the attached cable. 7) Do this task: Flight Data Recorder System - Activation, TASK 31-31-00-440-801. 8) Make sure that RECORDER field, on the DART screen, shows CONNECTED. 9) Make sure that function buttons, F1, F2, and F3, on the DART screen, are red in color. 10) Push the F3 button for MONITOR DATA on the DART screen. 11) Use the Up, Down, Left, and Right arrow buttons and Enter button to make the following selections on the DATA DEFINITION screen: a) Select Octal DATA FORMAT b) Select the applicable DATA RATE c) Select Normal DISPLAY MODE d) Select the applicable SYNC TYPE e) Select NO for the SUPER FRAME. 12) Push F4 button to select NEXT . 13) On the Data Word Definition screen, set the WORD and SUBFRAME as specified. a) For SUBFRAME 0, select ALL. 31-31-81 14) Push F4 button to select START MONITORING. 15) If necessary, enter the aircraft tail number. a) Use the Up, Down, Left, and Right arrow buttons to navigate around the keyboard. b) Push the Enter button to add the selected alphanumeric digit to the aircraft tail number text entry area. c) Push F4 button to select OK. SUBTASK 31-31-81-860-004 (2) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name C 9 C00109 FLIGHT RECORDER AC C 10 C00468 FLIGHT RECORDER DC SUBTASK 31-31-81-700-001 (3) Do this test of the flight data recorder accelerometer [1]: NOTE: For SUBFRAME 0, select ALL. (c) For Longitudinal Acceleration, set the portable tester to subframe 0, word 45. 1) Make sure that octal value 4031 to 4213 shows on the tester.

(c) Remove the tester from the Flight Data Recorder System (FDRS). (d) Do this task: Flight Data Recorder System - Activation, TASK 31-31-00-440-801. (e) If it is necessary, close access to the FDR (TASK 31-31-11-400-802). (f) If it is removed, install the shorting plug on the system test plug on the P18 panel.

A. General

(1) This procedure removes electrical power from the Aural Warning System.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Procedure

SUBTASK 31-51-00-860-180 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name E 3 C00170 MACH WARN SYS-1 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 7 C00210 FLIGHT CONTROL FLAP SHUTOFF VALVES A 21 C00396 FIRE PROT DETECTION MA WRN & CONT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 17 C00129 LANDING GEAR LATCH & PRESS WARN C 18 C01398 LANDING GEAR TAKEOFF WARNING CUTOFF D 18 C00451 LANDING GEAR AURAL WARN

D. Aural Warning System - Tryout

NOTE: This tryout is to make sure the Aural Warning System is in a zero energy state. SUBTASK 31-51-00-860-181 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are open and have safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-2 Row Col Number Name E 3 C00170 MACH WARN SYS-1 31-51-00 F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-2 Row Col Number Name A 7 C00210 FLIGHT CONTROL FLAP SHUTOFF VALVES A 21 C00396 FIRE PROT DETECTION MA WRN & CONT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name B 17 C00129 LANDING GEAR LATCH & PRESS WARN C 18 C01398 LANDING GEAR TAKEOFF WARNING CUTOFF D 18 C00451 LANDING GEAR AURAL WARN SUBTASK 31-51-00-700-001 (2) Do the following steps at the aural warning module: (a) Turn and hold the ROTATE TO TEST switch on top of the aural warning module to the A position. 1) Make sure you do not hear the intermittent horn. (b) Release the ROTATE TO TEST switch. 1) Make sure you do not hear the clacker. (c) Turn and hold the ROTATE TO TEST switch on top of the aural warning module to the B position. 1) Make sure you do not hear the intermittent horn. (d) Release the ROTATE TO TEST switch. 1) Make sure you do not hear the clacker.

31-51-00-740-801Aural Warning Module BITE Test ATA 31 · Task 740-801

Task Description

2. Aural Warning Module BITE Test NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) The BITE does an operational check of the two channels within the aural warning module. You need to do a test of channel A and channel B separately. Failure of this BITE test indicates a failure of the module only.

B. References

Reference Title 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201) 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Procedure

SUBTASK 31-51-00-860-001 (1) Do this task: Supply Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-811. SUBTASK 31-51-00-860-002 (2) Make sure that this circuit breaker is closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name D 18 C00451 LANDING GEAR AURAL WARN SUBTASK 31-51-00-740-001 (3) Do the channel A BITE test as follows: (a) Turn and hold the ROTATE TO TEST switch on top of the aural warning module to the A position. 1) Make sure you hear the intermittent horn. (b) Release the ROTATE TO TEST switch. 1) Make sure you hear the clacker for approximately 5 seconds. SUBTASK 31-51-00-740-002 (4) Do the channel B BITE test as follows: 31-51-00 (a) Turn and hold the ROTATE TO TEST switch on top of the aural warning module to the B position. 1) Make sure you hear the intermittent horn. (b) Release the ROTATE TO TEST switch. 1) Make sure you hear the clacker for approximately 5 seconds.

E. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 31-51-00-860-003 (1) Do this task: Remove Electrical Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-812.

31-51-00-730-802Landing Warning System Test ATA 31 · Task 730-802

Task Description

4. Landing Warning System Test NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

(1) The Landing Warning System (LWS) will come on if the airplane is in a landing configuration and any of the gear is not down and locked. The landing warning horn that you will hear is continuous. 31-51-00 (2) The LWS receives inputs from the gear down lock sensors, the landing gear lever position, the radio altimeters, the thrust lever position switches, the flap landing warning switch (S138), the Stall Warning Yaw Damper (SWYD) computers, the horn cutout switch and the engine running relays. (3) The continuous landing warning horn will sound when a gear is not down and locked and one of these conditions exist: (a) The trailing edge flaps are positioned from 0 to 10 units while one of the Thrust Lever Angle (TLA) is less than 21 degrees of thrust, the other TLA is at less than 34 degree of thrust and the radio altitude is less than 800 ft (244 m). NOTE: The horn can be silenced with the horn cutout switch only when the radio altitude is between 200 ft (61 m) and 800 ft (244 m). (b) The trailing edge flaps are positioned from 15 to 25 units with one of the TLA is at less than 21 degrees of thrust and the other TLA is at less than 34 degrees of thrust. The horn cannot be stopped with the horn cutout switch. NOTE: The thrust lever settings are different during a one engine landing. (c) The trailing edge flaps are at more than 25 units. The position of the TLAs does not matter. The horn cannot be stopped with the horn cutout switch.

B. References

Reference Title 10-11-05-500-801 Chock Installation in Winds or Wind Gusts to a Maximum of 35 Knots (P/B 201) 29-11-00-860-801 Hydraulic System A or B Pressurization (P/B 201) 29-11-00-860-805 Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal (P/B 201) 32-09-00-860-801 Put the Airplane in the Air Mode (P/B 201) 32-09-00-860-802 Return the Airplane to the Ground Mode (P/B 201) 34-33-00-700-801 Radio Altitude Simulation Test (P/B 201) 36-00-00-860-806 Remove Pressure from the Pneumatic System (P/B 201)

C. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. 31-51-00-730-803Takeoff Warning System Test ATA 31 · Task 730-803

Task Description

5. Takeoff Warning System Test NOTE: This procedure is a scheduled maintenance task.

A. General

RYR 169 PRE SB 737-31A1332 (1) The TAKEOFF CONFIG indicator lights installed on the P1-3 captains' instrument panel and the P3-1 first officer's instrument panel have been placarded inoperative. However, these indicator lights will come on when the lights switch on the captain's instrument panel P1-3 is set to the TEST position. 31-51-00 RYR 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-168, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 169 POST SB 737-31A1332 (2) TAKEOFF CONFIG indicator lights are installed on the P1-3 captains instrument panel, and the P3-1 first officer's instrument panel. They will come on when the takeoff warning horn comes on. RYR ALL (3) The takeoff warning system will come on if the airplane is not in a takeoff configuration and you move the thrust levers forward for takeoff. The takeoff warning horn that you will hear is intermittent. (4) The parking brake is released during the takeoff warning test. This prevents the parking brake function of the takeoff warning horn from overriding the tests. Put chocks on the landing gear wheels. (5) The intermittent takeoff warning horn will sound when the airplane is on the ground and you move one or both of the thrust levers forward for takeoff and at least one of these conditions exist: (a) The stabilizer is not in the green band (b) The trailing edge flaps are at less than 1 unit or more than 25 units (c) The leading edge flaps/slats are not in the extend or full extend position, or are in a UCM condition (d) The speedbrake handle is not down (e) The parking brake is set (f) The ground spoilers are not down (6) The intermittent takeoff warning horn will also sound when the airplane is in the air and all of the conditions below exist: (a) The ground spoiler valve is not closed (b) The leading edge flaps are not extended. (c) Make sure that this circuit breaker is closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-3 Row Col Number Name C 18 C01398 LANDING GEAR TAKEOFF WARNING CUTOFF (7) The takeoff warning system has these inputs: (a) A left throttle forward input from the left autothrottle switchpack, M1766 (b) A right throttle forward input from the right autothrottle switchpack, M1767 (c) Ground spoiler up pressure switch, S1049 (d) Ground spoiler interlock valve, S1050 (e) A logic input from the flap/slat electronics unit, M1746 (f) The speed brake switch, S651 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236 (g) The airplane nose up (stabilizer leading edge down) switch, S132 (h) The airplane nose down (stabilizer leading edge up) switch, S546 31-51-00 RYR 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999 (i) The airplane nose up (stabilizer leading edge down) switches, S132 & S1184 (j) The airplane nose down (stabilizer leading edge up) switches, S546 & S1183 RYR ALL (k) A parking brake input.

(1) The autothrottle switch pack replacement test does a return to service test for switches S1, S8, and S9 interface with the Proximity Switch Electronic Unit (PSEU).

31-51-01-820-801Flap Landing Warning Switch Adjustment and Test ATA 31 · Task 820-801

Task Description

4. Flap Landing Warning Switch Adjustment and Test (Figure 401, Figure 402)

A. References

Reference Title 20-10-44-400-801 Lockwire, Cotter Pins, and Lockrings - Installation (P/B 401) 29-11-00-860-801 Hydraulic System A or B Pressurization (P/B 201) 29-11-00-860-805 Hydraulic System A or B Power Removal (P/B 201) 29-22-00-710-801 Power Transfer Unit Operational Test (P/B 501) 32-00-01-080-801 Landing Gear Downlock Pins Removal (P/B 201) 32-00-01-480-801 Landing Gear Downlock Pins Installation (P/B 201)

B. Consumable Materials

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 134 Main Landing Gear Wheel Well, Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 727.00 - Right

D. Adjustment Procedure

SUBTASK 31-51-01-860-005 WARNING KEEP PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE FLIGHT CONTROL SURFACES, THE THRUST REVERSERS, AND THE LANDING GEAR. THESE COMPONENTS CAN MOVE SUDDENLY WHEN YOU SUPPLY HYDRAULIC POWER. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (1) Pressurize the hydraulic system for system B to 3000 psi (TASK 29-11-00-860-801). SUBTASK 31-51-01-220-002 (2) Move the flap control lever to the 15 unit position. SUBTASK 31-51-01-220-003 (3) Depressurize the hydraulic system for the system B (TASK 29-11-00-860-805). SUBTASK 31-51-01-220-004 (4) Position the flaps electrically until dimension X is 8.09 ± 0.03 inches. NOTE: The dimension X of 8.09 ± 0.03 inches corresponds to a flap position that is between 10 and 15 units. SUBTASK 31-51-01-820-002 (5) Adjust the flap landing warning switch [8]: (a) Turn the inner nut [12] to temporarily adjust the switch [8] in the direction of the cam [9] until you hear a click. (b) Turn the inner nut to move the switch away from the cam [9] until you hear a click. 31-51-01 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224 PRE SB 737-27-1290 AND PRE SB 737-27-1304 NOTE: There will be continuity between pins 1 and 2 on the limit switch. There will be continuity between pins 16 and 17 on the limit switch. (f) Remove the switch [1] from the bracket assembly [2].

NOTE: The adjustment gear must be adjusted when the switch is not loaded by the actuating cam. (b) Hand-adjust the stabilizer jackscrew to the necessary position so that the switch is not loaded. NOTE: Clockwise movement of the jackscrew crank results in a decrease in the “B” dimension. (c) Adjust the switch roller arm position on the switch spline shaft to the approximate switch actuation position. (d) Hand-tighten the locking nut [2] and non-removable lock screws [3]. (e) Rotate the stabilizer jackscrew until the switch actuates. NOTE: The audible “click” at the point of actuation is very subtle. The switch actuating can be sensed by placing your fingers on the switch housing while adjusting to feel the switch actuate. E. Speedbrake Takeoff Warning Switch Installation

SUBTASK 31-51-03-420-001 (1) Do these steps to install the speedbrake takeoff warning switch [6]: CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT THE ELECTRICAL WIRING ROUTING IS CORRECT . INCORRECT ROUTING OF THE WIRES CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE SWITCH CASE AND THE TERMINAL. (a) If it is necessary, connect the electrical wires terminals to the switch [6] before you tighten the switch [6] as follows: 1) Make sure that the electrical wiring routing is correct (View D, Figure 401). 2) If it is necessary, install shrinkable sleeve when you connect the electrical wires to switch [6] (SWPM 20-10-14). 3) Connect the wire No. 20 terminal [10] to the NC terminal. 4) Connect the wire No. 19 terminal [11] to the COM terminal. 5) Connect the wire No. 21 terminal [12] to the NO terminal. 6) Install the three screws [3] and lockwashers [4] on the switch [6]. (b) Put the switch [6] into position on the switch mount stud. (c) Install the washer [7] and self-locking nut [8] on the switch mount stud. 1) Do not tighten the self-locking nut [8] until you adjust the switch [6]. (d) Install the washer [7] and screw [9] on the switch [6]. SUBTASK 31-51-03-820-002 (2) Adjust the speedbrake takeoff warning switch [6]. (a) Disconnect the D10982 and D10984 plugs from the Proximity Sensor Electronics Unit (PSEU) before you adjust the switch position. (b) Lift the speedbrake lever out of the DOWN detent position and move it aft. 31-51-03 (c) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, between terminals COM and NC of the switch [6]. 1) Make sure that there is continuity between the terminals and no continuity between terminals COM and NO. (d) Hold the speedbrake lever forward and down in the detent, rotate the switch [6] counterclockwise until the roller [13] is fully depressed by the speedbrake lever actuator finger [5] (Figure 401). 1) Use zero gap condition. CAUTION DO NOT APPLY MORE THAN 4 IN-LB (0.4 N*M) OF TORQUE TO THE SELF-LOCKING NUT OR SCREW. TOO MUCH TORQUE WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT . (e) Tighten the screw [9] and self-locking nut [8] to 2 in-lb (0.2 N·m) - 3 in-lb (0.3 N·m) above run-on torque. (f) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, between terminals COM and NO of the switch [6]. 1) Make sure that there is continuity between the terminals. 2) If there is no continuity, do these steps: a) Loosen the screw [9] and self-locking nut [8]. b) Re-adjust the switch [6]. (g) Hold the speedbrake lever forward, raise the speedbrake lever to its internal stop. (h) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, between terminals COM and NO of the switch [6]. 1) Make sure that there is no continuity between the terminals. (i) Move the speedbrake lever slightly aft and slowly lower it into the detent position. 1) Maintain contact between the speedbrake lever dog and the aft side of the detent position. (j) Make sure that switch [6] meets the following circuit conditions BEFORE the lever reaches the fully down position. (k) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, between terminals COM and NO of the switch [6]. 1) Make sure that there is continuity between the terminals. (l) Move the reverse thrust lever to the REVERSE THRUST position. (m) Connect the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793, between terminals COM and NO of the switch [6]. 1) Make sure that there is no continuity between the terminals. (n) Remove the digital/analog multimeter, COM-1793. (o) Move the reverse thrust lever to the stow position. SUBTASK 31-51-03-410-001 (3) Put the switch cover [1] into its position on the control stand. SUBTASK 31-51-03-410-002 (4) Install the screws [2]. 31-51-03 SUBTASK 31-51-03-420-005 (5) Connect the D10982 and D10984 plugs to the PSEU. SUBTASK 31-51-03-420-003 (6) Do this task: Stabilizer Trim Control Wheel Installation, TASK 27-41-61-410-801. Speedbrake Installation Test SUBTASK 31-51-03-860-005 (1) Make sure that the airplane is in the ground mode. (a) If it is necessary, do this task: Return the Airplane to the Ground Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-802. SUBTASK 31-51-03-860-013 WARNING MAKE SURE THAT YOU OPEN THE CIRCUIT BREAKER FOR THE WEATHER RADAR SYSTEM. THE FORWARD MOVEMENT OF A THRUST LEVER CAN CAUSE THE AUTOMATIC OPERATION OF THE SYSTEM. THE OPERATION OF THIS SYSTEM CAN CAUSE SERIOUS INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT IN THE AREA OF THE NOSE RADOME. (2) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-1 Row Col Number Name D 13 C00120 WEATHER RADAR RT SUBTASK 31-51-03-420-004 WARNING KEEP PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE FLIGHT CONTROL SURFACES, THE THRUST REVERSERS, AND THE LANDING GEAR. (3) Pressurize the hydraulic systems A and B, do this task: Hydraulic System A or B Pressurization, TASK 29-11-00-860-801. (b) Make sure that the stabilizer is within 1 unit of the center of the green band. (c) Make sure that the speedbrake lever is in the DOWN detent position. 31-51-03 (d) Release the parking brakes. (e) Set the thrust lever no. 1 to the full forward thrust position. SUBTASK 31-51-03-730-004 (7) Lift the speedbrake lever from the DOWN detent position without moving the lever aft. (a) Make sure that you hear an intermittent horn. SUBTASK 31-51-03-730-002 (8) Set the speedbrake lever to the UP position. (a) Make sure that the intermittent horn continues. SUBTASK 31-51-03-730-003 (9) Put the speedbrake lever in the DOWN detent position. (a) Make sure that the intermittent horn stops.
